### ALL-PET: A Low-resource and Low-shot PET Foundation Model in the Projection Domain
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09130v1)
- **Authors**: Bin Huang, Kang Chen, Bingxuan Li, Huafeng Liu, Qiegen Liu
- **Abstract**: Building large-scale foundation model for PET imaging is hindered by limited access to labeled data and insufficient computational resources. To overcome data scarcity and efficiency limitations, we propose ALL-PET, a low-resource, low-shot PET foundation model operating directly in the projection domain. ALL-PET leverages a latent diffusion model (LDM) with three key innovations. First, we design a Radon mask augmentation strategy (RMAS) that generates over 200,000 structurally diverse training samples by projecting randomized image-domain masks into sinogram space, significantly improving generalization with minimal data. This is extended by a dynamic multi-mask (DMM) mechanism that varies mask quantity and distribution, enhancing data diversity without added model complexity. Second, we implement positive/negative mask constraints to embed strict geometric consistency, reducing parameter burden while preserving generation quality. Third, we introduce transparent medical attention (TMA), a parameter-free, geometry-driven mechanism that enhances lesion-related regions in raw projection data. Lesion-focused attention maps are derived from coarse segmentation, covering both hypermetabolic and hypometabolic areas, and projected into sinogram space for physically consistent guidance. The system supports clinician-defined ROI adjustments, ensuring flexible, interpretable, and task-adaptive emphasis aligned with PET acquisition physics. Experimental results show ALL-PET achieves high-quality sinogram generation using only 500 samples, with performance comparable to models trained on larger datasets. ALL-PET generalizes across tasks including low-dose reconstruction, attenuation correction, delayed-frame prediction, and tracer separation, operating efficiently with memory use under 24GB.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ALL-PET paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ALL-PET, a low-resource and low-shot foundation model for PET imaging.  It operates directly on sinogram (projection) data, which preserves valuable physical priors absent in image-domain approaches. ALL-PET uses a latent diffusion model (LDM) augmented with three key innovations: (1) Radon Mask Augmentation Strategy (RMAS) and Dynamic Multi-Mask (DMM) mechanism to generate diverse training samples from limited data; (2) Positive/Negative mask constraints to enforce geometric consistency, and (3) Transparent Medical Attention (TMA), a parameter-free, geometry-driven mechanism that enhances lesion-related regions in raw projection data using coarse lesion segmentation. The model is shown to perform well on tasks such as low-dose reconstruction, attenuation correction, delayed-frame prediction, and tracer separation, requiring only 500 training samples and operating with low computational resources (under 24GB memory usage). The authors demonstrate superior performance over existing generative models and show the utility of synthetic data generated from ALL-PET for improving downstream tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates some significant novelty in several areas:
    *   *Projection Domain Operation:*  Most medical imaging foundation models operate in the image domain. Directly working with sinograms is a clear departure and offers advantages by explicitly incorporating physical priors inherent in the projection data, leading to improved consistency and interpretability.
    *   *Data Augmentation & Masking:* The RMAS and DMM mechanisms are clever ways to exponentially increase training data diversity from a limited source. The use of Radon transforms to create geometrically consistent masks is particularly interesting and tailored to the PET imaging problem. The positive/negative constraint is also clever.
    *   *Transparent Medical Attention (TMA):* This is a strong contribution. The concept of leveraging coarse segmentation to drive attention in the projection domain is novel and clinically relevant. The parameter-free nature and clinician control further enhance its utility.

*   **Significance & Impact:** The paper addresses critical challenges hindering the deployment of foundation models in PET imaging: data scarcity and high computational cost. By significantly reducing the data and resource requirements, ALL-PET has the potential to democratize access to advanced PET imaging tools.
    *   *Low Resource Performance:* The model achieves competitive results with significantly fewer training samples (500 vs tens or hundreds of thousands) and much lower GPU memory requirements than other foundation models.
    *   *Downstream Task Generalization:*  The ability of ALL-PET to generalize across different tasks (reconstruction, correction, prediction, separation) without task-specific retraining highlights its versatility.
    *   *Clinical Relevance:* Focus on physical consistency and clinician interpretability (TMA) make it more likely to be adopted.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong methodological innovation in data augmentation and attention mechanisms tailored for PET.
    *   Clear demonstration of low-resource and low-shot capabilities.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation with quantitative and qualitative results.
    *   Addresses a critical gap in PET imaging research.
    *   Open-source code and data availability (github).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited data types and sizes. The data comes from a single center and a relatively small number of patients.  While the paper demonstrates success with a small dataset, the true generalizability across different scanners, radiotracers, and patient populations needs more rigorous validation. Multi-center evaluation is key to demonstrating robustness.
    *   Reliance on FBP/OSEM for ROI generation. The reliance on thresholded FBP or OSEM reconstructions for TMA introduces potential bias and user dependence. Although clinician defined ROI helps with bias, the paper does not discuss inter-reader or intra-reader variabilities in the ROI specification. More robust, automated methods for ROI extraction could improve the model's reliability and clinical applicability.
    *   Deeper discussion of the "physical priors" is needed. The paper refers to physical priors, but could go into more detail on *which* priors are explicitly encoded and how their preservation leads to better results compared to image-domain approaches.
    *   Comparison to other sinogram based methods lacking. A comparison to other non-foundation model approaches that also operate in the sinogram domain could strengthen the evaluation.
    *   Lack of runtime analysis for various components. The paper mentions memory usage and provides information for certain experimental phases, but not the overall training time. Providing a runtime analysis could showcase ALL-PET's practical performance more effectively.

*   **Score Justification:**

The paper presents a compelling and novel approach to foundation modeling in PET imaging by operating in the sinogram domain and addressing data scarcity with innovative data augmentation strategies. The TMA mechanism introduces clinically relevant interpretability. While the single-center dataset and need for more rigorous multi-center validation are limitations, the potential impact of democratizing PET imaging with low-resource models is significant.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Adaptive Pareto-Optimal Token Merging for Edge Transformer Models in Semantic Communication
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09168v1)
- **Authors**: Omar Erak, Omar Alhussein, Hatem Abou-Zeid, Mehdi Bennis
- **Abstract**: Large-scale transformer models have emerged as a powerful tool for semantic communication systems, enabling edge devices to extract rich representations for robust inference across noisy wireless channels. However, their substantial computational demands remain a major barrier to practical deployment in resource-constrained 6G networks. In this paper, we present a training-free framework for adaptive token merging in pretrained vision transformers to jointly reduce inference time and transmission resource usage. We formulate the selection of per-layer merging proportions as a multi-objective optimization problem to balance accuracy and computational cost. We employ Gaussian process-based Bayesian optimization to construct a Pareto frontier of optimal configurations, enabling flexible runtime adaptation to dynamic application requirements and channel conditions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method consistently outperforms other baselines and achieves significant reductions in floating-point operations while maintaining competitive accuracy across a wide range of signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) conditions. Additional results highlight the effectiveness of adaptive policies that adjust merging aggressiveness in response to channel quality, providing a practical mechanism to trade off latency and semantic fidelity on demand. These findings establish a scalable and efficient approach for deploying transformer-based semantic communication in future edge intelligence systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a training-free framework for adaptive token merging in pre-trained vision transformers deployed at the edge for semantic communication. The goal is to jointly reduce inference time and communication resource usage without retraining the model. The approach formulates token merging proportion selection at each layer as a multi-objective optimization problem, balancing task accuracy and computational cost. Gaussian Process-based Bayesian optimization is used to construct a Pareto frontier of optimal configurations. Experiments demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms other baselines, achieves significant FLOP reductions while maintaining competitive accuracy under various signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) conditions, and can dynamically adjust merging aggressiveness in response to channel quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its combination of training-free token merging (building upon ToMe) with Bayesian optimization for creating a Pareto front specifically tailored for edge semantic communication. While token merging is not a new concept, applying it adaptively *and* training-free in the context of semantic communication with edge devices, using multi-objective Bayesian optimization, is a novel contribution.  Specifically, the use of norm-based weighted merging and the dynamic selection of token merging configurations based on channel conditions and Pareto optimality differentiates it from prior art. However, individually, components such as ToMe and Bayesian optimization, are established approaches, which limits the overall novelty score.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in deploying large transformer models at the edge: computational cost. By offering a training-free solution that can significantly reduce FLOPs while maintaining accuracy, the work makes a practical contribution toward enabling transformer-based semantic communication in resource-constrained 6G networks. The adaptive nature of the framework, allowing dynamic trade-offs between accuracy and efficiency based on channel conditions, is particularly valuable. The construction of a Pareto front offers a practical method for real-time adjustments based on network conditions or application priorities. However, the experimental scope is somewhat limited to image classification tasks. Further evaluation on diverse datasets and tasks (like object detection or semantic segmentation as the authors mention in the future work), would significantly strengthen the significance. The reliance on a pretrained transformer (while simplifying the setup) also doesn't explore the interaction between pretraining objectives and the token merging process. The JSCC also lacks detail, and if a simple baseline is used, the results could be significantly affected if a more advanced JSCC were implemented.

* **Strengths:**
    * Training-free approach: Avoids the need for expensive retraining.
    * Multi-objective optimization:  Allows flexible trade-offs between accuracy and efficiency.
    * Adaptive merging: Adjusts to changing channel conditions.
    * Strong experimental results: Demonstrates significant performance improvements over baselines.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Limited experimental scope: Focuses mainly on ImageNet classification.
    * JSCC could be better described and a more advanced JSCC could improve results
    * Pretrained Transformer only: Limited to pretrained transformers and does not study pretraining objectives and impact on merging
    * Limited theoretical analysis.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of edge intelligence systems by demonstrating a practical and efficient way to deploy transformer models in resource-constrained environments. The Pareto front approach can inspire further research on adaptive and resource-aware deep learning techniques for edge computing. The simplicity and adaptability of the method make it a good candidate for further exploration and practical implementation.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the identified strengths and weaknesses, a score of 7 is appropriate. The paper presents a novel combination of techniques to address a relevant problem, demonstrating promising experimental results. However, the incremental nature of the individual components, the limited scope of the experiments, JSCC implementation, and no discussion of the impact of different pretrained models, prevent it from achieving a higher score. The Pareto optimal configuration has the potential to significantly impact semantic communication systems.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### EchoX: Towards Mitigating Acoustic-Semantic Gap via Echo Training for Speech-to-Speech LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09174v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhao Zhang, Yuhao Du, Zhanchen Dai, Xiangnan Ma, Kaiqi Kou, Benyou Wang, Haizhou Li
- **Abstract**: Speech-to-speech large language models (SLLMs) are attracting increasing attention. Derived from text-based large language models (LLMs), SLLMs often exhibit degradation in knowledge and reasoning capabilities. We hypothesize that this limitation arises because current training paradigms for SLLMs fail to bridge the acoustic-semantic gap in the feature representation space. To address this issue, we propose EchoX, which leverages semantic representations and dynamically generates speech training targets. This approach integrates both acoustic and semantic learning, enabling EchoX to preserve strong reasoning abilities as a speech LLM. Experimental results demonstrate that EchoX, with about six thousand hours of training data, achieves advanced performance on multiple knowledge-based question-answering benchmarks. The project is available at https://github.com/FreedomIntelligence/EchoX.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the "EchoX: Towards Mitigating Acoustic-Semantic Gap via Echo Training for Speech-to-Speech LLMs" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the intelligence degradation observed in speech-to-speech large language models (SLLMs) compared to text-based LLMs. The authors hypothesize that this degradation is due to the acoustic-semantic gap in feature representation space, which they aim to mitigate with a novel "Echo Training" approach called EchoX. EchoX uses semantic representations to dynamically generate speech training targets, integrating acoustic and semantic learning.  The method involves pre-training a text-to-speech codec (T2C) model, leveraging the T2C module to decode the outputs of the S2T LLM as training targets, and introducing a denoise adapter to align the representational spaces.  Experiments demonstrate improved performance on knowledge-based question-answering benchmarks with a relatively smaller dataset. The paper also explores unit language modeling for long-sequence processing and introduces a streaming inference mechanism.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The Echo Training strategy is the most novel aspect of the paper. The idea of using a pre-trained T2C model to generate pseudo-labels for speech training, forcing the SLLM to learn consistent acoustic-semantic mappings, is a valuable contribution. The introduction of a Denoising Adapter is also a nice addition. However, the use of unit language modeling and streaming inference, while practically beneficial, are not entirely novel concepts in the field. The incremental novelty of incorporating the Echo Decoder is less clear, making the contribution appear relatively modest.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in tackling a crucial problem in SLLMs: the degradation of reasoning abilities. While current approaches focus on scale (more data, larger models), EchoX offers a more targeted solution by explicitly addressing the acoustic-semantic gap. The experimental results, though limited, are promising, showing improvements on question-answering tasks even with fewer training resources. If the results hold up with larger datasets and more rigorous testing, this approach could be an important stepping stone in building more intelligent and resource-efficient SLLMs.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and hypothesis.
    *   Well-defined approach with a clear explanation of the Echo Training strategy.
    *   Demonstrated improvement on knowledge-based question-answering benchmarks.
    *   The method is potentially more data-efficient than simply scaling up the training data.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited experimental evaluation. The benchmarks used, while relevant, need to be complemented with a broader range of tasks and datasets, especially considering the focus on long sequences and real-time performance.
    *   The specific benefits and impact of the denoise adapter are unclear and need further quantification.
    *   Lack of a robust ablation study. It is important to test all modules of the proposed method by removing them to verify their significance to the final performance.
    *   While mentioning unit language and streaming inference, the paper lacks details on their implementation and interaction with the core EchoX framework.
    *   Lack of consideration for factors beyond the acoustic-semantic gap. While this is a major challenge, it is not the only one.
    *   Comparisons are largely at a high level. It would be helpful to dig more into how the models respond to the questions and where the differences lie.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the results are validated with more thorough experimentation and larger models, EchoX could influence future research by promoting more targeted training strategies that focus on aligning different modalities. The echo training methodology can become a standard training pipeline for SLLMs.

**Score:** 6.0

**Rationale:** While the core idea of Echo Training is promising and addresses a significant challenge in SLLMs, the limited experimental validation and lack of detailed analysis prevent a higher score. The reliance on existing techniques (unit language modeling) and the lack of ablation studies also diminish the overall contribution. Nevertheless, the paper presents a valuable contribution by offering a potential solution to the "intelligence degradation" problem in SLLMs and providing a blueprint for future research.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### AI Reasoning for Wireless Communications and Networking: A Survey and Perspectives
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09193v1)
- **Authors**: Haoxiang Luo, Yu Yan, Yanhui Bian, Wenjiao Feng, Ruichen Zhang, Yinqiu Liu, Jiacheng Wang, Gang Sun, Dusit Niyato, Hongfang Yu, Abbas Jamalipour, Shiwen Mao
- **Abstract**: Artificial Intelligence (AI) techniques play a pivotal role in optimizing wireless communication networks. However, traditional deep learning approaches often act as closed boxes, lacking the structured reasoning abilities needed to tackle complex, multi-step decision problems. This survey provides a comprehensive review and outlook of reasoning-enabled AI in wireless communication networks, with a focus on Large Language Models (LLMs) and other advanced reasoning paradigms. In particular, LLM-based agents can combine reasoning with long-term planning, memory, tool utilization, and autonomous cross-layer control to dynamically optimize network operations with minimal human intervention. We begin by outlining the evolution of intelligent wireless networking and the limitations of conventional AI methods. We then introduce emerging AI reasoning techniques. Furthermore, we establish a classification system applicable to wireless network tasks. We also present a layer-by-layer examination for AI reasoning, covering the physical, data link, network, transport, and application layers. For each part, we identify key challenges and illustrate how AI reasoning methods can improve AI-based wireless communication performance. Finally, we discuss key research directions for AI reasoning toward future wireless communication networks. By combining insights from both communications and AI, this survey aims to chart a path for integrating reasoning techniques into the next-generation wireless networks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

This paper provides a survey of AI reasoning techniques, specifically focusing on their application within wireless communications and networking. It emphasizes the limitations of traditional AI approaches (like deep learning) in handling complex, real-world network scenarios, arguing for the need for reasoning-enabled AI, particularly leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs). The survey presents a classification of reasoning methods (prompting strategies, architectural innovations, and learning paradigms) and then examines how these methods can be applied at different layers of the network stack (physical, data link, network, transport, and application). The paper concludes by outlining future research directions, including agentic AI reasoning, data-efficient learning, efficient deployment of large models, and safe/reliable AI systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The paper offers a relatively comprehensive overview of both AI reasoning techniques and the challenges/opportunities within wireless networking.  It covers a wide range of relevant literature.
    *   **Layered Approach:**  Organizing the discussion by network layer provides a clear and structured way to understand the specific challenges and potential AI reasoning solutions for each layer. This approach is valuable for researchers and practitioners.
    *   **Emphasis on Emerging Trends:** The focus on LLMs and other advanced reasoning paradigms is timely and relevant, reflecting current trends in both AI and wireless communications.
    *   **Future Directions:** The paper identifies several promising future research avenues, providing a roadmap for further exploration in the field.
    *   The comparison of the various AI reasoning methods in table 2 and a discussion of the tradeoffs in each method are very useful.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Depth in Specific Applications:** While the paper provides a broad overview, it sometimes lacks depth in specific application examples. It could benefit from more detailed case studies or real-world implementation scenarios to illustrate the practical impact of AI reasoning in wireless networks.
    *   **Overly Optimistic Tone:** The paper generally presents a positive view of AI reasoning's potential.  A more critical discussion of the limitations, challenges, and potential pitfalls of deploying these techniques in real-world wireless environments would be beneficial. For example, practical constraints for applying LLMs, data requirements, computational cost, and edge deployment were only mentioned superficially and required more in-depth discussion.
    *   **Lack of Quantitative Results:** The survey primarily focuses on qualitative descriptions.  The inclusion of quantitative results from existing studies comparing AI reasoning methods to traditional approaches would strengthen the claims made in the paper.
    *   **Novelty:** There are already surveys available on AI/LLMs for wireless networks as well as surveys on AI reasoning. The authors claim to bridge the gap by providing a reasoning-aware AI/LLM survey that specifically covers wireless applications and organizes the discussion by network layers. Although the contribution and effort of writing the survey are evident, the degree of the novelty compared with the existing surveys is somewhat limited and incremental.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper's significance lies in its attempt to bridge the gap between the AI reasoning research community and the wireless communications networking community. By providing a structured overview of reasoning techniques and their potential applications in wireless, it can facilitate cross-disciplinary collaboration and accelerate the development of more intelligent and adaptive wireless networks.  The identification of future research directions also helps to guide future work in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **6.5** to this paper.

*   The paper demonstrates a solid understanding of both AI reasoning and wireless networking, presenting a well-structured survey of the field. The survey can act as a good starting point for researchers who seek to gain a quick understanding of using LLMs in different layers of wireless networks.
*   However, the limited depth of coverage, and somewhat optimistic tone reduces the level of novelty. It would benefit from a more critical discussion of the limitations of AI reasoning in wireless environments, and lacks quantitative support for the claims.
*   Therefore, it makes a valuable contribution to the field.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.NI
- **Score**: 6/10

### On Integrating Large Language Models and Scenario-Based Programming for Improving Software Reliability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09194v1)
- **Authors**: Ayelet Berzack, Guy Katz
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are fast becoming indispensable tools for software developers, assisting or even partnering with them in crafting complex programs. The advantages are evident -- LLMs can significantly reduce development time, generate well-organized and comprehensible code, and occasionally suggest innovative ideas that developers might not conceive on their own. However, despite their strengths, LLMs will often introduce significant errors and present incorrect code with persuasive confidence, potentially misleading developers into accepting flawed solutions. In order to bring LLMs into the software development cycle in a more reliable manner, we propose a methodology for combining them with ``traditional'' software engineering techniques in a structured way, with the goal of streamlining the development process, reducing errors, and enabling users to verify crucial program properties with increased confidence. Specifically, we focus on the Scenario-Based Programming (SBP) paradigm -- an event-driven, scenario-based approach for software engineering -- to allow human developers to pour their expert knowledge into the LLM, as well as to inspect and verify its outputs. To evaluate our methodology, we conducted a significant case study, and used it to design and implement the Connect4 game. By combining LLMs and SBP we were able to create a highly-capable agent, which could defeat various strong existing agents. Further, in some cases, we were able to formally verify the correctness of our agent. Finally, our experience reveals interesting insights regarding the ease-of-use of our proposed approach. The full code of our case-study will be made publicly available with the final version of this paper.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "On Integrating Large Language Models and Scenario-Based Programming for Improving Software Reliability."

**Summary**

The paper proposes a methodology for integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) with Scenario-Based Programming (SBP) to improve software reliability.  The core idea is to leverage LLMs for code generation while using SBP's structured approach to guide the LLM, verify its outputs, and inject human domain expertise.  The methodology involves defining scenarios, providing background knowledge to the LLM, and iteratively developing scenario threads with refinement through testing and verification. The approach is evaluated through a Connect4 case study, demonstrating that an LLM-guided SBP agent can achieve strong performance, and partial correctness can be formally verified. The authors also discuss their experience interacting with the LLM and highlight the benefits and challenges of their approach.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:*

The paper's novelty lies in the specific combination of LLMs with SBP in a structured methodology aimed at improving software reliability. While LLMs and SBP are not individually novel, the synthesis of the two with a focus on human guidance, iterative refinement, and formal verification represents a distinct contribution. Prior work has explored LLMs for code generation and SBP for system design, but the explicit methodology and the empirical evaluation using Connect4, including formal verification aspects, provide a valuable insight. The extensions to the SBP semantics, allowing for event-specific priorities, are also a novel element. However, it's important to acknowledge that this work builds upon existing research on LLM-based code generation and scenario-based techniques. Also, the idea of using LLMs with more structured programming paradigms like SBP is natural and might have been thought of by others.

*Significance:*

The paper addresses a crucial problem: the unreliability of code generated by LLMs. The proposed methodology offers a promising approach to mitigate this issue by integrating LLMs into a more controlled and verifiable development process. The Connect4 case study provides empirical evidence that the method can lead to effective agents with some verifiable properties.  The lessons learned from the development process, specifically the importance of human guidance, iterative refinement, and clear prompts, are valuable for other researchers and practitioners working with LLMs. The extension of SBP semantics allows for greater control over event handling, which is significant in handling specific types of problems such as competing priorities and the ability to override blocking scenarios.

*Strengths:*

*   **Clear Methodology:** The paper presents a well-defined and structured methodology that is easy to understand and follow.
*   **Empirical Evaluation:** The Connect4 case study provides a concrete example of how the methodology can be applied in practice.
*   **Formal Verification:** The inclusion of formal verification helps to establish some degree of confidence in the correctness of the generated code.
*   **Detailed Discussion:** The paper provides a detailed discussion of the challenges and benefits of the approach, as well as practical insights into working with LLMs.
*   **SBP Extension:** The extension to SBP event handling provides a way to solve a common problem that frequently appears in real-life applications.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is limited to a single case study, which may not be representative of all software development tasks.
*   **Partial Correctness:** The formal verification only provides partial correctness guarantees, and the agent is not proven to be perfect.
*   **Domain Specificity:** The Connect4 game is a somewhat artificial domain, and it is unclear how well the methodology would generalize to more complex and realistic software systems.
*   **LLM Dependence:** The methodology is still heavily reliant on the capabilities of the LLM, and its performance may vary depending on the LLM used.
*   **Limited Empirical comparisons** The empirical comparison should also include more comparisons. It is claimed that the results are better than various state-of-the-art agents, but there are no formal quantitative analysis or comparisons.
*   **Subjective Developer Experience:** While the paper provides insights into the development process, the assessment of developer experience is somewhat subjective and lacks quantitative data.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical and verifiable approach to integrating LLMs into software development. It can inspire other researchers to explore similar hybrid methodologies that combine the strengths of LLMs with traditional software engineering techniques. The extension of SBP semantics is also a valuable contribution that may find applications in other contexts.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty of the specific integration of LLMs and SBP, the significance of the problem addressed, the empirical evidence provided, and the detailed discussion of the development process, but also acknowledging the limited scope of the evaluation and the partial correctness guarantees, a score of **7** is appropriate.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enabling Regulatory Multi-Agent Collaboration: Architecture, Challenges, and Solutions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09215v1)
- **Authors**: Qinnan Hu, Yuntao Wang, Yuan Gao, Zhou Su, Linkang Du
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs)-empowered autonomous agents are transforming both digital and physical environments by enabling adaptive, multi-agent collaboration. While these agents offer significant opportunities across domains such as finance, healthcare, and smart manufacturing, their unpredictable behaviors and heterogeneous capabilities pose substantial governance and accountability challenges. In this paper, we propose a blockchain-enabled layered architecture for regulatory agent collaboration, comprising an agent layer, a blockchain data layer, and a regulatory application layer. Within this framework, we design three key modules: (i) an agent behavior tracing and arbitration module for automated accountability, (ii) a dynamic reputation evaluation module for trust assessment in collaborative scenarios, and (iii) a malicious behavior forecasting module for early detection of adversarial activities. Our approach establishes a systematic foundation for trustworthy, resilient, and scalable regulatory mechanisms in large-scale agent ecosystems. Finally, we discuss the future research directions for blockchain-enabled regulatory frameworks in multi-agent systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the governance and accountability challenges arising from the increasing use of LLM-powered autonomous agents in collaborative environments.  It proposes a blockchain-enabled, layered architecture for regulatory multi-agent collaboration, comprising an agent layer, a blockchain data layer, and a regulatory application layer. The architecture incorporates three key modules: (1) agent behavior tracing and arbitration via smart contracts, (2) dynamic reputation evaluation for trust assessment, and (3) malicious behavior forecasting using diffusion models for early detection of adversarial activities. The goal is to establish a trustworthy, resilient, and scalable regulatory foundation for large-scale agent ecosystems.  The paper also presents experimental results demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed solutions and outlines future research directions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its integrated approach combining blockchain technology, smart contracts, reputation systems, and diffusion models to address the specific challenges of regulating LLM-based multi-agent systems. While each component (blockchain, smart contracts, reputation, diffusion models) is not entirely novel in isolation, their combined application to the regulation of *autonomous* agents with LLMs represents a significant contribution. The introduction of diffusion models for *proactive* adversarial behavior detection is particularly interesting. The architecture itself, with its layered approach, is a reasonable design choice given the problem space, though not fundamentally groundbreaking. The connection to regulatory compliance in agents is fresh.

*   **Significance:** The increasing prevalence of autonomous agents, especially those powered by LLMs, necessitates robust regulatory mechanisms. This paper tackles a critical issue for the safe and ethical deployment of these technologies. The potential impact is high, as effective regulation could unlock greater adoption and benefits from multi-agent systems across various industries. The proposed architecture offers a practical blueprint for building such regulatory systems. If successful in practice, this architecture could be a starting point for many. However, there are assumptions such as the quality of data being input into the models, the ease of deployment, and scalability.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges associated with regulating LLM-based agent collaboration.
    *   **Comprehensive Architecture:** The proposed architecture provides a well-defined framework for addressing these challenges.
    *   **Integrated Solution:** The combination of blockchain, smart contracts, reputation, and diffusion models is a novel and promising approach.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The experimental results demonstrate the feasibility and effectiveness of the proposed solutions, although the scope of the experiments may be limited.
    *   **Discussion of Future Directions:** The paper outlines important areas for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Experimental Scope:** While the experiments show promise, they may not fully capture the complexities of real-world multi-agent ecosystems, especially regarding scale and diverse adversarial behaviors. More extensive evaluations are needed.
    *   **Scalability Concerns:** The paper does not adequately address scalability challenges associated with using blockchain for large-scale agent networks.  Blockchain transactions can be costly and slow. Performance in a high transaction environment could be an issue.
    *   **LLM Uncertainty:** LLMs are known for being unpredictable and hallucinating. If LLMs are influencing any stage of detection, there will be an increase in false positives.
    *   **Security of Smart Contracts:** Smart contracts are code and can be subject to exploits. If there are exploits in the smart contracts for behaviors tracing and arbitration, it will provide a bad reputation or shut the agents down.

*   **Impact and Influence:**  The paper has the potential to significantly influence the design and implementation of regulatory frameworks for multi-agent systems. It introduces innovative techniques for enforcing accountability, assessing trust, and detecting malicious behavior. The open-source nature of the blockchain should lower the barrier to entry and provide the means to test and improve. Further research and development building upon this work could pave the way for more trustworthy and resilient agent ecosystems.

*   **Overall:** This paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of multi-agent systems regulation by combining cutting-edge technologies to address critical challenges. While improvements in experimental validation, scalability, and security would strengthen the work, the proposed architecture and solutions offer a promising foundation for future research and development in this area.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The score reflects the paper's significant novelty in integrating various technologies for autonomous agent regulation, its high potential impact on ensuring trustworthy agent collaboration, and its reasonably well-defined architecture and solutions. The limitations in experimental scope and the need to address scalability and security concerns prevent it from achieving a higher score. A score of 7.5 appropriately positions it as a solid and valuable contribution that requires further refinement and validation to fully realize its potential.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Reading Between the Lines: Classifying Resume Seniority with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09229v1)
- **Authors**: Matan Cohen, Shira Shani, Eden Menahem, Yehudit Aperstein, Alexander Apartsin
- **Abstract**: Accurately assessing candidate seniority from resumes is a critical yet challenging task, complicated by the prevalence of overstated experience and ambiguous self-presentation. In this study, we investigate the effectiveness of large language models (LLMs), including fine-tuned BERT architectures, for automating seniority classification in resumes. To rigorously evaluate model performance, we introduce a hybrid dataset comprising both real-world resumes and synthetically generated hard examples designed to simulate exaggerated qualifications and understated seniority. Using the dataset, we evaluate the performance of Large Language Models in detecting subtle linguistic cues associated with seniority inflation and implicit expertise. Our findings highlight promising directions for enhancing AI-driven candidate evaluation systems and mitigating bias introduced by self-promotional language. The dataset is available for the research community at https://bit.ly/4mcTovt
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for classifying resume seniority.  The authors introduce a hybrid dataset combining real-world resumes with synthetically generated "hard examples" designed to mimic overstated or understated experience.  They evaluate the performance of zero-shot GPT-4 and fine-tuned BERT-based models against a traditional TF-IDF logistic regression baseline. Results show that fine-tuned BERT models outperform zero-shot GPT-4, suggesting the value of task-specific fine-tuning in this domain. The work aims to address the challenge of accurately assessing candidate seniority in the face of biased self-presentation in resumes. The study concludes that LLMs, especially when fine-tuned, show promise for improving AI-driven candidate evaluation systems and mitigating bias.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Hybrid Dataset:**  The creation of a hybrid dataset is a significant contribution. The inclusion of synthetically generated hard examples specifically designed to test the models' ability to detect subtle manipulations in self-presentation is novel and addresses a key weakness of relying solely on real-world data, which may not adequately represent the full spectrum of seniority biases. This enhances the robustness of the evaluation. The authors make the dataset available.
    * **Rigorous Evaluation:** The paper provides a comparative evaluation of various LLM architectures (GPT-4, DistilBERT, RoBERTa) and contrasts them with a traditional machine learning baseline. This multi-faceted approach strengthens the credibility of the findings.
    * **Focus on Bias Mitigation:** The explicit focus on mitigating bias introduced by self-promotional language is a relevant and important consideration, particularly in the context of fair hiring practices. This highlights the societal impact of the research.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written, clearly outlining the methodology, results, and conclusions.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Limited Scope of Synthetic Data:**  While the synthetic data is a strength, the prompts used to generate the variations are relatively simplistic. It's unclear if the generated text fully captures the subtle nuances of real-world resume writing and self-promotion strategies. The variety of prompts used could be expanded for more robust testing.
    * **Generalizability Concerns:** The study acknowledges that findings may not directly translate to broader domains but doesn't deeply explore the specific factors that could limit generalizability (e.g., industry-specific jargon, cultural differences in resume writing).
    * **Limited Exploration of LLM Explainability:** While the paper mentions using chain-of-thought prompting, it doesn't delve deeply into analyzing the explanations provided by the models to understand *why* certain classifications were made. This could provide valuable insights into the models' reasoning processes and potential biases.
    * **Comparison to Recent Literature:** While the paper addresses recent advances, comparisons against other contemporary techniques in this domain are limited, especially regarding different prompt engineering strategies and advanced LLM architectures released recently.

* **Novelty and Significance:**

    The novelty lies primarily in the creation and use of the hybrid dataset for evaluating LLMs in the context of resume seniority classification, particularly the incorporation of synthetically generated hard examples. This moves beyond simply applying LLMs to existing resume datasets and directly addresses the challenge of detecting subtle linguistic manipulations. The significance is in the potential to improve the fairness and accuracy of AI-driven hiring tools by mitigating bias and enhancing the ability to identify qualified candidates regardless of their self-presentation skills. The contribution is valuable, but it relies on established methods and has limitations in scope and sophistication.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by providing a novel approach to evaluate LLMs for resume seniority classification, especially concerning the mitigation of biases stemming from self-promotional content. The hybrid dataset is a valuable asset. However, the limitations related to the scope of synthetic data generation, generalizability concerns, and limited explainability analysis temper the overall impact. While the paper is well-executed and clearly presented, it doesn't represent a breakthrough discovery or fundamentally alter the direction of research in the field. Thus, a rating between 5 and 7 seems appropriate. Given the strengths of the study (particularly the hybrid dataset), a score of 6.5 is assigned.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Agentic LLMs for Question Answering over Tabular Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09234v1)
- **Authors**: Rishit Tyagi, Mohit Gupta, Rahul Bouri
- **Abstract**: Question Answering over Tabular Data (Table QA) presents unique challenges due to the diverse structure, size, and data types of real-world tables. The SemEval 2025 Task 8 (DataBench) introduced a benchmark composed of large-scale, domain-diverse datasets to evaluate the ability of models to accurately answer structured queries. We propose a Natural Language to SQL (NL-to-SQL) approach leveraging large language models (LLMs) such as GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini, and DeepSeek v2:16b to generate SQL queries dynamically. Our system follows a multi-stage pipeline involving example selection, SQL query generation, answer extraction, verification, and iterative refinement. Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach, achieving 70.5\% accuracy on DataBench QA and 71.6\% on DataBench Lite QA, significantly surpassing baseline scores of 26\% and 27\% respectively. This paper details our methodology, experimental results, and alternative approaches, providing insights into the strengths and limitations of LLM-driven Table QA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents an agentic Natural Language to SQL (NL-to-SQL) pipeline for question answering over tabular data (Table QA). It addresses the challenges posed by diverse real-world table structures and query complexities. The proposed system leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) like GPT-40, GPT-40-mini, and DeepSeek v2:16b to dynamically generate SQL queries. The pipeline consists of example selection, SQL query generation, answer extraction, verification, and iterative refinement stages. The system was evaluated on the SemEval 2025 Task 8 (DataBench) benchmark, demonstrating significant accuracy improvements over baseline scores.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Pipeline:** The multi-stage pipeline is a significant strength. The inclusion of example selection, verification, and iterative refinement helps to address the common issues of hallucination and errors in LLM-generated SQL.
*   **Performance on DataBench:** The reported accuracy on the DataBench and DataBench Lite datasets showcases the practical effectiveness of the approach. The large margin by which the paper surpasses baseline methods (26-27% to 70-71% for GPT-4o) strongly supports the viability of this architecture.
*   **Detailed Methodology:** The paper clearly outlines each stage of the pipeline, including prompting strategies, data formatting, and error-handling mechanisms. This provides valuable insights for replication and further development.
*   **Model Comparison:** The comparison of different LLMs (GPT-40, GPT-40-mini, DeepSeek v2:16b) provides useful insights into the strengths and limitations of each model within this specific task.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is well-executed, the individual components (LLMs, SQL generation, verification) are not entirely novel. The main contribution lies in the integration of these existing techniques into a robust pipeline.
*   **Dependency on Powerful LLMs:** The system relies heavily on the capabilities of powerful LLMs like GPT-40. This raises concerns about accessibility and cost, particularly for applications with limited resources. The DeepSeek v2:16b results highlight this, as they improve over the baseline substantially but are still well behind GPT-4o results.
*   **Lack of Error Analysis:** While the paper reports accuracy improvements, it would benefit from a more detailed error analysis. Identifying the types of queries where the system still struggles would provide valuable guidance for future research directions. The authors note complex numerical reasoning and categorical misclassification as areas of failure, but do not do more than mention this.
*   **Limited Evaluation Scope:** The evaluation is primarily focused on the DataBench benchmark. Evaluating the system on other Table QA datasets would further demonstrate its generalizability.
*   **Modest Advance:** The individual gains introduced by these techniques on their own is difficult to assess. While the whole is greater than the sum of its parts, it is unclear *how much* better the whole is over the isolated parts.

**Significance:**

The paper demonstrates the potential of LLM-driven NL-to-SQL pipelines for Table QA. It showcases how a well-designed architecture can significantly improve accuracy by addressing the challenges of query generation, verification, and refinement. The results are practically significant, especially for enterprise applications that rely on extracting information from diverse databases. The methodology provides a valuable reference point for researchers working in this area.

**Score:** 6.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable and practically relevant application of LLMs to Table QA. The reported accuracy improvements are compelling, and the detailed methodology is well-documented. However, the overall novelty is somewhat limited, as the individual techniques have been explored in previous work. The reliance on powerful LLMs raises concerns about accessibility and cost, and the lack of in-depth error analysis and evaluation scope limit the impact of the paper. Given the strong focus on empirical results and practical contributions, the score reflects this. The core idea is not revolutionary, but it implements existing ideas quite well and reaches significantly improved results.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Jupiter: Enhancing LLM Data Analysis Capabilities via Notebook and Inference-Time Value-Guided Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09245v1)
- **Authors**: Shuocheng Li, Yihao Liu, Silin Du, Wenxuan Zeng, Zhe Xu, Mengyu Zhou, Yeye He, Haoyu Dong, Shi Han, Dongmei Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown great promise in automating data science workflows, but existing models still struggle with multi-step reasoning and tool use, which limits their effectiveness on complex data analysis tasks. To address this, we propose a scalable pipeline that extracts high-quality, tool-based data analysis tasks and their executable multi-step solutions from real-world Jupyter notebooks and associated data files. Using this pipeline, we introduce NbQA, a large-scale dataset of standardized task-solution pairs that reflect authentic tool-use patterns in practical data science scenarios. To further enhance multi-step reasoning, we present Jupiter, a framework that formulates data analysis as a search problem and applies Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to generate diverse solution trajectories for value model learning. During inference, Jupiter combines the value model and node visit counts to efficiently collect executable multi-step plans with minimal search steps. Experimental results show that Qwen2.5-7B and 14B-Instruct models on NbQA solve 77.82% and 86.38% of tasks on InfiAgent-DABench, respectively-matching or surpassing GPT-4o and advanced agent frameworks. Further evaluations demonstrate improved generalization and stronger tool-use reasoning across diverse multi-step reasoning tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces JUPITER, a novel framework designed to enhance the data analysis capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). It addresses the limitations of existing LLMs in handling complex, multi-step data analysis tasks by combining a new large-scale dataset (NbQA) derived from real-world Jupyter notebooks with an inference-time value-guided search strategy. NbQA provides standardized task-solution pairs reflecting authentic tool-use in data science, while JUPITER employs Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) and a value model to efficiently explore and generate diverse solution trajectories. Experiments demonstrate that LLMs fine-tuned on NbQA and guided by JUPITER achieve significant improvements in multi-step reasoning, tool use, and generalization across various data analysis benchmarks, often surpassing proprietary models and advanced agent frameworks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a combination of novel elements. Constructing a large-scale dataset (NbQA) derived from Jupyter notebooks is a significant contribution, providing a valuable resource for training and evaluating data analysis capabilities of LLMs. The integration of MCTS with a value model for inference-time search in the context of notebook-based data analysis is also a relatively new approach. While MCTS and value functions are well-established, their application to LLM-augmented data analysis with a dataset created directly from notebook corpora is a novel combination. However, value guided search has been explored extensively across many domains, suggesting that this is more of an incremental advance.
*   **Significance:** The findings demonstrate a tangible improvement in LLMs' ability to tackle complex data analysis tasks. The fact that open-source models, when enhanced with JUPITER, can match or even outperform proprietary systems like GPT-4 is a significant outcome. This has implications for democratizing data analysis by empowering users with more accessible and capable tools. The evidence presented is strong, including improvements over baseline models and comparisons with existing frameworks on several benchmarks. The generalizability of the approach across different tasks (DSBench, AIME) further strengthens the significance of the work. However, improvements on out-of-domain tasks such as the AIME benchmark appear limited.
*   **Strengths:** The paper is well-written, clearly structured, and supported by thorough experimentation. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component (NbQA, MCTS, value model). The comparison with state-of-the-art agent systems demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The paper also addresses potential limitations, such as the reliance on rule-based rewards in reinforcement learning and potential future directions for improvement. The creation of a large and valuable resource (NbQA) also bolsters the importance of this research.
*   **Weaknesses:** While the experimental results are convincing, a more in-depth analysis of the types of errors that JUPITER helps LLMs avoid would be beneficial. Additionally, further exploration of the limitations of the value model, especially in out-of-domain scenarios, would enhance the analysis. The improvements on out-of-domain tasks appear limited, with no gains beyond multiple sampling on the AIME benchmark. The limited improvement in such settings tempers the paper's significance. The reliance on GPT-4 to generate the NbQA dataset raises questions about dataset bias.
*   **Potential Influence:** The JUPITER framework and the NbQA dataset could influence future research directions in LLM-augmented data analysis. Other research efforts might adopt the methodology for creating datasets from real-world notebooks, and the value-guided search strategy could be applied to other domains. The paper highlights that open-source models can be pushed to achieve results competitive with proprietary ones, driving the field forward.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, a score of 7 is appropriate. The paper combines existing techniques in a novel way to address a relevant problem and presents convincing experimental evidence. The creation of the NbQA dataset is a significant contribution that can enable further research efforts. However, the incremental advance to existing value guided search techniques and limited out-of-domain transfer prevent this paper from being a breakthrough contribution.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### DATE: Dynamic Absolute Time Enhancement for Long Video Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09263v1)
- **Authors**: Chao Yuan, Yang Yang, Yehui Yang, Zach Cheng
- **Abstract**: Long video understanding remains a fundamental challenge for multimodal large language models (MLLMs), particularly in tasks requiring precise temporal reasoning and event localization. Existing approaches typically adopt uniform frame sampling and rely on implicit position encodings to model temporal order. However, these methods struggle with long-range dependencies, leading to critical information loss and degraded temporal comprehension. In this paper, we propose Dynamic Absolute Time Enhancement (DATE) that enhances temporal awareness in MLLMs through the Timestamp Injection Mechanism (TIM) and a semantically guided Temporal-Aware Similarity Sampling (TASS) strategy. Specifically, we interleave video frame embeddings with textual timestamp tokens to construct a continuous temporal reference system. We further reformulate the video sampling problem as a vision-language retrieval task and introduce a two-stage algorithm to ensure both semantic relevance and temporal coverage: enriching each query into a descriptive caption to better align with the vision feature, and sampling key event with a similarity-driven temporally regularized greedy strategy. Our method achieves remarkable improvements w.r.t. absolute time understanding and key event localization, resulting in state-of-the-art performance among 7B and 72B models on hour-long video benchmarks. Particularly, our 7B model even exceeds many 72B models on some benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DATE (Dynamic Absolute Time Enhancement), a novel method to improve temporal reasoning in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for long video understanding. DATE addresses the limitations of existing approaches that rely on uniform frame sampling and implicit position encodings, which struggle with long-range dependencies and precise temporal localization.  DATE incorporates two main components: (1) a Timestamp Injection Mechanism (TIM) that interweaves video frame embeddings with textual timestamp tokens, creating a continuous temporal reference system, and (2) a Temporally-Aware Similarity Sampling (TASS) strategy. TASS reformulates video sampling as a vision-language retrieval task and employs a two-stage algorithm that ensures both semantic relevance and temporal coverage. The paper demonstrates that DATE achieves state-of-the-art performance on hour-long video benchmarks, particularly in tasks requiring absolute time understanding and key event localization, even surpassing some 72B models with a 7B model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper introduces a genuinely novel approach to address the challenges of temporal reasoning in MLLMs for long videos.  The Timestamp Injection Mechanism (TIM) and Temporally-Aware Similarity Sampling (TASS) are innovative contributions. TIM provides explicit and controllable temporal references, which is an improvement over implicit position encodings that can degrade with sequence length. TASS's reformation of video sampling as a vision-language retrieval task, combined with semantic-guided caption generation, is also a novel strategy for selecting relevant frames. The two-stage sampling algorithm enhances semantic relevance and temporal diversity effectively.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in its ability to improve temporal reasoning and event localization in MLLMs for long videos. By enhancing temporal awareness, DATE enables more accurate understanding of video content, particularly in tasks that require precise timing. The experimental results demonstrate substantial performance gains compared to existing approaches. Notably, DATE's smaller 7B model outperforms larger 72B models on some benchmarks, highlighting the efficiency of the method. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of the TIM and TASS components.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of temporal reasoning in MLLMs for long videos.
*   **Innovative Approach:** The DATE method is a novel and well-designed solution to address the identified challenges.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results demonstrate significant performance improvements on multiple challenging benchmarks.
*   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of the individual components.
*   **Comparisons with SOTA:** Comprehensive comparisons with state-of-the-art methods demonstrate the superiority of DATE.
*   **Well-written and well-structured:** The paper is clearly written and organized, making it easy to understand.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** The reliance on frame-level similarity computation in TASS may introduce computational overhead, particularly for extremely long videos. While the authors acknowledge this limitation, future work could explore more efficient sampling strategies.
*   **Bad Cases:** There are indications that some illusion issues appear when the additional tokens take the capacity of model.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of MLLMs for video understanding. The proposed DATE method offers a promising solution for improving temporal reasoning and event localization, which are critical for understanding long video content. The paper's findings could inspire further research on efficient and accurate temporal modeling techniques for MLLMs.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents a well-defined problem, a novel solution with a clear methodology, solid empirical validation across several standard benchmark datasets, and an in-depth analysis. The method is thoroughly evaluated, its superiority confirmed compared to existing and baseline implementations.
Its approach to timestamping and temporally-aware sampling significantly boosts the performance of long-video understanding capabilities.

### Harnessing Uncertainty: Entropy-Modulated Policy Gradients for Long-Horizon LLM Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09265v1)
- **Authors**: Jiawei Wang, Jiacai Liu, Yuqian Fu, Yingru Li, Xintao Wang, Yuan Lin, Yu Yue, Lin Zhang, Yang Wang, Ke Wang
- **Abstract**: In long-horizon tasks, recent agents based on Large Language Models (LLMs) face a significant challenge that sparse, outcome-based rewards make it difficult to assign credit to intermediate steps. Previous methods mainly focus on creating dense reward signals to guide learning, either through traditional reinforcement learning techniques like inverse reinforcement learning or by using Process Reward Models for step-by-step feedback. In this paper, we identify a fundamental problem in the learning dynamics of LLMs: the magnitude of policy gradients is inherently coupled with the entropy, which leads to inefficient small updates for confident correct actions and potentially destabilizes large updates for uncertain ones. To resolve this, we propose Entropy-Modulated Policy Gradients (EMPG), a framework that re-calibrates the learning signal based on step-wise uncertainty and the final task outcome. EMPG amplifies updates for confident correct actions, penalizes confident errors, and attenuates updates from uncertain steps to stabilize exploration. We further introduce a bonus term for future clarity that encourages agents to find more predictable solution paths. Through comprehensive experiments on three challenging agent tasks, WebShop, ALFWorld, and Deep Search, we demonstrate that EMPG achieves substantial performance gains and significantly outperforms strong policy gradient baselines. Project page is at https://empgseed-seed.github.io/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Entropy-Modulated Policy Gradients (EMPG), a novel reinforcement learning (RL) framework designed to improve the training of Large Language Model (LLM) agents in long-horizon tasks with sparse rewards. The core idea is to re-calibrate the policy gradient based on the agent's intrinsic uncertainty, as measured by the entropy of the generated tokens at each step. EMPG amplifies updates for confident, correct actions, penalizes confident errors (hallucinated confidence), and attenuates updates for uncertain steps, stabilizing exploration.  Additionally, EMPG includes a "future clarity bonus" that encourages actions leading to more predictable subsequent states.  The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of EMPG on challenging agent tasks like WebShop, ALFWorld, and Deep Search, showing substantial performance gains compared to strong policy gradient baselines (GRPO and DAPO).  A theoretical analysis is presented to justify the approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to address the long-horizon credit assignment problem in LLM agents. While entropy modulation has been explored in some contexts, the specific application to multi-step decision-making, the theoretical grounding in the dynamics of policy gradients, and the combination of gradient scaling and future clarity bonus differentiate it from prior work. The identification of the inherent coupling between gradient magnitude and policy entropy and its consequences is a key contribution. The self-calibrating gradient scaling mechanism provides a novel adaptive way to modulate the learning signal.

*   **Significance:** The paper's findings are significant for several reasons:

    *   It addresses a critical challenge in training LLM agents for complex tasks.
    *   It provides a practical and scalable framework (EMPG) that can be easily integrated with existing RL algorithms.
    *   The empirical results demonstrate substantial performance improvements on established benchmarks.
    *   The theoretical justification provides a deeper understanding of the underlying learning dynamics.
    *   The analysis of the learning dynamics and ablation studies provides valuable insights for future research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Strong empirical results on multiple challenging benchmarks.
    *   Well-motivated approach with a clear theoretical underpinning.
    *   Clear and well-written paper.
    *   Thorough analysis and ablation studies.
    *   Addresses a relevant and important problem.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on policy entropy as the sole measure of uncertainty might be limiting. While the paper acknowledges alternative uncertainty estimators, it does not explore them extensively.
    *   The "future clarity bonus" is somewhat heuristic, although theoretically justified. Further exploration of its relationship to information gain and empowerment could strengthen the approach.
    *   While EMPG consistently improves performance, the improvement on the already strong DAPO baseline on WebShop is relatively small (though still statistically significant), suggesting potential limitations in particularly complex environments.
    *   The implementation details could be more detailed.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in several areas:

    *   It can inspire new approaches for credit assignment in long-horizon tasks.
    *   It can encourage further investigation of the relationship between policy uncertainty and learning dynamics.
    *   It can provide a practical framework for training more effective LLM agents.
    *   It highlights the value of leveraging intrinsic signals for self-supervision.

*   **Justification for Score:**  The paper presents a well-motivated and empirically validated approach to a significant problem in the field of LLM agents. While the approach is not without limitations, the novelty, significance, and potential influence justify a strong score. The clarity of the presentation and the thoroughness of the analysis further contribute to its value.

### Fusing Knowledge and Language: A Comparative Study of Knowledge Graph-Based Question Answering with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09272v1)
- **Authors**: Vaibhav Chaudhary, Neha Soni, Narotam Singh, Amita Kapoor
- **Abstract**: Knowledge graphs, a powerful tool for structuring information through relational triplets, have recently become the new front-runner in enhancing question-answering systems. While traditional Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) approaches are proficient in fact-based and local context-based extraction from concise texts, they encounter limitations when addressing the thematic and holistic understanding of complex, extensive texts, requiring a deeper analysis of both text and context. This paper presents a comprehensive technical comparative study of three different methodologies for constructing knowledge graph triplets and integrating them with Large Language Models (LLMs) for question answering: spaCy, Stanford CoreNLP-OpenIE, and GraphRAG, all leveraging open source technologies. We evaluate the effectiveness, feasibility, and adaptability of these methods by analyzing their capabilities, state of development, and their impact on the performance of LLM-based question answering. Experimental results indicate that while OpenIE provides the most comprehensive coverage of triplets, GraphRAG demonstrates superior reasoning abilities among the three. We conclude with a discussion on the strengths and limitations of each method and provide insights into future directions for improving knowledge graph-based question answering.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper presents a comparative study of three different methods for constructing knowledge graph triplets and integrating them with Large Language Models (LLMs) for question answering (QA): spaCy, Stanford CoreNLP-OpenIE, and GraphRAG. The authors evaluate these methods based on several parameters, such as ease of use, performance, accuracy, and customizability, using Shakespeare's "All's Well That Ends Well" and the RepliQA dataset. The results indicate that while OpenIE provides the most comprehensive triplet coverage, GraphRAG demonstrates superior reasoning abilities, and spaCy offers a lightweight, high-precision baseline.  The paper concludes with a discussion on the strengths and limitations of each method and provides insights into future directions for improving knowledge graph-based question answering.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Comparison:** The paper offers a structured and comprehensive comparison of three popular KG-based QA approaches under identical conditions, addressing a notable gap in the literature.  The technical comparison using various metrics (installation, ease of use, documentation quality, customization) provides practical insights for researchers and practitioners.
    * **Reproducibility:** The framework is designed to be fully reproducible, which is a significant strength, enabling other researchers to verify and extend the findings.  The authors utilize open-source technologies, enhancing accessibility.
    * **Nuanced Performance Analysis:**  The performance evaluation goes beyond basic metrics (like Exact Match) by incorporating domain expert judgments and automated scoring (GPT-4) to assess the coherence, reasoning ability, and contextual richness of the answers.  The use of both human and automated evaluation adds credibility to the findings.
    * **Practical Recommendations:** The paper provides actionable recommendations for selecting optimal extraction techniques, balancing factors like factual coverage, reasoning depth, and computational efficiency.
    * **Discussion of Future Directions:** The paper identifies promising future research avenues, including dynamic graph updates, hybrid extraction pipelines, and multi-modal knowledge integration.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Dataset Diversity:** While the use of Shakespeare's play and RepliQA dataset is valuable, expanding the evaluation to include datasets from different domains (e.g., medical, legal, scientific) would further strengthen the generalizability of the findings. The datasets are small and could impact the findings.
    * **Lack of Quantitative Metrics:** While the paper qualitatively discusses various evaluation parameters, a greater emphasis on quantitative metrics (e.g., precision, recall, F1-score for triplet extraction, runtime analysis) would provide a more rigorous and objective assessment.
    * **Scalability Analysis:** A thorough scalability analysis, including performance comparisons on large-scale datasets and computational cost, is lacking. The paper mentions computational efficiency but doesn't provide concrete performance numbers.  This is crucial considering the varying resource demands of each method.
    * **Lack of Ablation Studies:** It would be beneficial to include ablation studies to assess the individual contributions of each component within the frameworks (e.g., the impact of different LLMs on GraphRAG, the effect of various dependency parsing techniques on spaCy's performance).
    * **Limited Baselines:** The paper compares three KG-QA frameworks. Including comparisons to a traditional RAG baseline would better illustrate the advantages of KG-QA approaches.

* **Novelty and Significance:**
   The paper's novelty lies in its comprehensive comparison of three popular KG-QA methodologies using both automated and human evaluation, offering an actionable guide for researchers and practitioners in this rapidly evolving field. By conducting a fully reproducible study and systematically examining each method across a wide range of parameters, the paper addresses a gap in existing literature that tends to focus on individual methodologies in isolation or rely on less robust evaluation strategies. However, it is not extremely groundbreaking in its methods or discoveries.

* **Potential Influence:**
   The paper is expected to be highly influential within the NLP and Information Retrieval communities due to its practical guidance for KG-QA system design.

**Justification for Score:**
The paper provides a systematic and reproducible comparison of KG-based QA techniques, which is valuable. However, the limitations in quantitative analysis, limited dataset diversity, and the absence of comparison with baseline RAG techniques lowers the score somewhat. While the practical recommendations and identification of future directions enhance its impact, the limitations prevent it from achieving a higher score.
